[1;38;5;78mThe wish, and the four things it asks

Wave 43 died mid-flight on a weekly
account limit, not on a defect. It
completed 36 of 41 agents, produced ten

pushed NOTHING. The lead recovered four
branches out of their worktrees by hand
and opened #9332, #9333, #9334, #9335 at

ran; its debrief was never written.
Those four PRs are UNREVIEWED BY ANYONE,
and their commit messages are an
author's own account of their own work.

The wish asks for four things: (1) re-
derive #9332's live write bypass from
source before trusting a word of it; (2)
verify the other three the same way; (3)
confirm PDS-D633's measurement law - the
in-BEAM meter is blind to port children
and errs in the one direction a price

the price column: N of 19 instruments
under a required gate, every remaining
refusal carrying a measured price, a
named environment, or NOT-YET-BUILT.

The finished experience: at the end of

that appears in the charter next wave is
a number nobody typed.

[1;38;5;59mMovement 1 - LAND THE RECOVERED WORK,
WITH INDEPENDENCE THAT IS REAL[0m

Charter #9312 merges FIRST and alone,
because until it does every D633+
citation a builder would carry points at
nothing on origin/main. Then the four
slice PRs, each with an independent re-
derivation that does NOT read the
branch's own test as its oracle. #9332
gets the full treatment: the source-
level order defect is already confirmed
(cond arm 1 = caps.write, arm 2 =
restricted?, and restricted? already
contains share_access == :read), so the
open question is REACHABILITY - can a
real signed-in NON-MEMBER, through a
real non-admin route, hold share_access:
:read and caps.write == true
simultaneously? That is the whole claim,
and it must be re-derived through
LiveScope.assign_scope ->
authorize_read/4, not asserted from a
fixture the branch authored.

One suspected defect the wave carries in
already, found while reading the diff
and NOT yet proven: #9332's membership
collapse routes through
loadable_principal?/1, which accepts
only %ApiToken{} and %User{}.
Tenancy.Auth.authorize/3 on origin/main
has a THIRD clause at :200 for
%Barkpark.Content.CallerContext{}. If a
socket's principals list can ever carry
a CallerContext, the collapse silently
drops a principal that authorize/3 would
have decided - a behaviour change
dressed as a round-trip saving, in the
same slice as a security fix. Survey
question 3 exists to settle it before a
builder inherits it.

[1;38;5;59mMovement 3 - THE DOOR CENSUS: the price
column stops being prose[0m

This is the bold one. PDS-D637's column
is, today, forty lines of charter prose
sitting on an unmerged PR - and PDS-
D633's own closing sentence is that a
number from a meter must live in the
instrument's moduledoc and printed
output, NEVER in prose a copy-paste can
drop. The column violates the law that
produced it. Wave 44 turns it into an
instrument: enumerate scripts/pds-
*.{sh,exs} for the denominator; for each
row DERIVE the gate status from two
independent legs - leg A, a
'../../../scripts/...' string literal in
an api/test/** file that actually shells
it; leg B, that same path present in
elixir-path-escape-check.sh's declared
set - and report THROUGH only when BOTH
hold. One leg without the other is
exactly the wave-42 #9290/#9292 defect
(Test skipped while Elixir gate passed
in 3s on the skip shim), and this makes
that defect impossible to reintroduce
quietly rather than merely remembered.

Every non-THROUGH row carries a class
from D637's FIVE (not three) and, for
PRICE rows, a figure with the meter
named. An instrument with no disposition
is UNDISPOSED and the census exits non-
zero - the same fail-closed shape the
receipt census already uses. It prints
D633's blind-spot sentence in its own
output. And it rides its own door: it is
an instrument, so it gets a test file
and a path-set line like every other,
which is the self-referential proof that
the derivation works.

[1;38;5;78mDirection debate - the rivals, and why
this one won[0m

[1;38;5;59mRival A - RECOVERY ONLY[0m

Spend the whole wave making wave 43's
four branches independently bulletproof
and merged, plus the charter, and ship
nothing new. The case for it is strong
and honest: an unreviewed authorization
change is the single riskiest object in
the repo right now, and a wave that
merges it carelessly while chasing new
capability has failed at the only thing
that mattered. It was rejected because
it under-uses the wave, not because it
is wrong: four PRs of independent re-
derivation is roughly one round, and the
fold plus the ladder are DESIGNED work
with their obligations already derived
by run - leaving them unbuilt for a
second consecutive wave is how wave 43's
best output rots. Rival A's real
contribution survives: movement 1 goes
first, alone, and no slice PR merges on
its own author's oracle.

[1;38;5;59mRival B - ADVANCE ONLY[0m

Treat the four branches as inherited
fact, merge them on their green, and
spend the wave taking the door from 3 of
19 to 6 or 7. Rejected outright: it is
precisely the move the wish forbids, and
the epic's own law forbids it too. A
commit message is an exit code with
better prose. Merging #9332 on its own
test's word is 'a gate lies when its
green costs nothing to produce',
committed by the wave that coined the
sentence.

[1;38;5;59mRival C - THE DOOR CENSUS (winner, as
movement 3 of three)[0m

The winning insight is that the epic has
spent four waves arguing about a column
it has never computed. Every wave
restates the roster, re-prices some
rows, and writes the total into the
charter by hand - and PDS-D636 records
that the census itself carries a FALSE
SENTENCE about its own gating on main
today. That is the exact failure mode
this epic exists to end, committed by
the epic, about the epic. Making the
column an instrument is the epic's own
law turned inward one more notch, and it
is cheap: the derivation is two greps
over files that already exist, and the
prior art is already on main
(api/test/barkpark/pds_residue_lens_test
.exs).

[1;38;5;59mThe sharpest attack on the winner, and
how the wave absorbs it[0m

THE ATTACK: the door census is a THIRD
census. This epic already has a receipt
census, a residue lens, a ledger census,
a record-parity arm and a charter-ledger
sweep - nineteen instruments, three of
which run anywhere. Building a twentieth
instrument to count the nineteen is the
purest possible instance of an epic
measuring itself instead of shipping.
And it will be WRONG on day one: leg A
('a test file shells it') has at least
three encodings in the repo already
(System.cmd with a module attribute,
Code.require_file, a bare relative
path), so a naive derivation will under-
count and print a number LOWER than the
truth - a different lie, in a friendlier
direction.

HOW THE WAVE ABSORBS IT. First, the door
census is movement THREE of three - it
ships only if movements 1 and 2 are
honest, and the wave is graded on the
security fix and the merges regardless.
Second, the under-count objection
becomes the instrument's design: the
census is FAIL-CLOSED on ambiguity - a
pds-* script it cannot dispose is
UNDISPOSED and reds, and a test-side
reference it cannot classify is an
ERROR, never a silent 'not gated'. That
makes the day-one wrongness LOUD instead
of quiet, and the surveyors are tasked
(question 4) with enumerating every
encoding of leg A that exists on main
before a builder writes a regex. Third,
if the survey shows the derivation
cannot be made honest cheaply, the
fallback is smaller and still real: a
self-check that the two legs AGREE for
every already-gated instrument - which
alone would have caught #9290 and #9292.

[1;38;5;78mThe survey plan - 20 assignments, and
what each one changes[0m

Width is the point. Each surveyor is one
Opus at medium effort; the digest
distils. Every load-bearing assumption
in the direction above appears below as
a question, including the ones that
would kill it.

[38;5;59m• [0m#9332's premise is REAL AND REACHABLE,
  derived independently by three
  surveyors from source.
  LiveScope.authorize_read/4 offers the
  :docs share arm (live_scope.ex:147)
  BEFORE the grant arm (:157), so a
  signed-in NON-MEMBER holding an active
  write grant on a :docs-shared desk is
  graded :share_read - share_access:
  :read + readonly_gate?: true, with
  NEITHER scope_to_grants read-narrowing
  NOR Access.validate write-narrowing
  armed. Meanwhile Caps.derive/1 loads
  active grants UNCONDITIONALLY
  (caps.ex:148-168, no grade reference)
  and Access.admits_desk?/3
  (access.ex:329-336) OVERWRITES the
  desk scope's :type and :doc_id with
  the grant's own before validating, so
  a one-doc grant auto-satisfies its own
  narrowest rungs. caps.write == true
  and share_access == :read co-occur.
  Order defect confirmed.

COVERAGE, INCLUDING NOT-FOUNDS. Reached
for the first time:
docs/decisions/success-claim-census.md,
Ops.commit/3, the sheet-grid palette
harness, and every leg-A encoding on
main. STILL NOT REACHED:
api/lib/barkpark/plugins/sheets/web/ops_
controller.ex:69, a SECOND
Session.apply_ops surface over HTTP that
the LiveView wall does not cover -
nobody examined it, and it is filed
rather than assumed safe. The SheetGrid
write-LANDING half is UNPROVEN, not
proven-safe: Session.peek returned
{:error, :no_session} in BOTH arms, so
an assertion there today would be
vacuous in both directions. Both are
backlog rows, not silent gaps.

[1;38;5;78mDecisions - PDS-D643 through D652, each
with its one-line why[0m

These mirror the charter (PR #9353); the
charter is the epic's memory, this Paper
is the wave's story. Because that PR may
not be on main while the builders fly,
the decisions are here in full.

[38;5;59m• [0mD643 - Movement 1 landed WHILE wave 44
  verified it, charter last. Why: 13
  dangling citations shipped, and
  #9334's green was a zero-second skip
  shim with ZERO gate legs - this epic's
  own law broken on this epic's own PR.
[38;5;59m• [0mD644 - #9332 is sound, its suspected
  own defect is void, and the bypass is
  NOT closed for the pure grant grade.
  Why: the narrowing hook is
  :handle_event and the component route
  is handle_info, so Access.validate/3
  is never consulted. Denying
  readonly_posture? would be the WRONG
  fix - the narrowing must travel into
  the door.
[38;5;59m• [0mD645 - The fold is recoverable, not
  rebuildable, and git-grep cannot see
  stranded work. Why: uncommitted work
  is the only kind that CAN be stranded,
  so any git-based sweep systematically
  misses exactly what it is looking for.
[38;5;59m• [0mD646 - 210 s is leaf CPU, not a gate
  budget. Why: a required gate enforces
  WALL, the driver is serial and prints
  nothing until the last case returns,
  and an in-flight run is
  indistinguishable from a hang by
  stdout alone.
[38;5;59m• [0mD647 - The ledger-census door is re-
  priced and 'viable' is dead. Why:
  42.6-49.3 s CPU and 144 checks, and
  the count is load-independent so this
  is staleness, not mismeasurement.
[38;5;59m• [0mD648 - Wall is not a measurable
  property of a door; the unit is CPU =
  user + sys, labelled LOCAL. Why: a
  FIXED workload swung 5.8x at constant
  load while its user CPU held to 27%,
  and on the hetzner door sys EXCEEDS
  user, understating it 2.1x as a pure
  CPU fact.
[38;5;59m• [0mD649 - The agreement check cannot
  fail; leg A must be attribute-bound
  AND executed. Why: leg A implies leg B
  by an existing required ratchet, and a
  comment-blind extractor lets one
  comment line plus the declaration the
  ratchet forces manufacture a THROUGH.
[38;5;59m• [0mD650 - The denominator is 19 and the
  inventory's owner is the INSTRUMENT'S
  OWN PRINTED OUTPUT. Why: no doc owns
  the fact-topic, the repo caps cards at
  7, and an appendix would push success-
  claim-census.md past its budget.
[38;5;59m• [0mD651 - The palette red is a harness
  attribution bug, not a contrast
  defect. Why: a generated :root block
  was inserted ABOVE the dark block, so
  an ordinal read compares dark hues
  against WHITE - and the standing task
  about it would send a builder to
  change a hue that passes at 7.27:1.
[38;5;59m• [0mD652 - What wave 44 cut, with reasons:
  the ledger-census door (price), the
  charter sweep (45 hand rulings, gated
  on this charter merging, and its
  second read FAILS OPEN), and the
  sheet-grid reader half (D641,
  deliberate).

[1;38;5;78mThe wave plan - 7 slices, 4 in round 1[0m

Round 1 is dependency-free and builds
this run. Round 2 does NOT build now:
each names the merge it waits on, stated
as the first line of its own brief so a
manually-dispatched builder sees it
first. Round-1 file sets are disjoint -
the census, the escape-check,
shared/paper.ex and the palette harness
are each owned by exactly one slice.

[1;38;5;78mWhat the review FOUND that the wave did
not report[0m

[38;5;59m• [0mPDS-D653 - A TASK CAN BE 11/12 STAMPED
  AND ITS BRANCH EMPTY. pds-w44-door-
  census-instrument stamped eleven
  criteria with real run evidence, its
  now-line read 'committing: ...
  branching loop-epic/the-price-column-
  stops-being-charter-pro-2', and git
  diff origin/main...that-branch is
  EMPTY. It was in NEITHER the built
  handoff nor the not-green list. The
  rule this buys: the LEDGER is the
  sweep index, not the handoff - diff
  every wave task's stated branch
  against origin/main and treat an empty
  diff as STRANDED, because a report
  cannot describe the builder that
  stopped before writing it.
[38;5;59m• [0mPDS-D654 - A GATE THAT DOES NOT
  TRIGGER ON ITS OWN SUBJECT IS GREEN
  FOR FREE. sheet-grid-js.yml runs a
  harness that parses root.html.heex,
  presence_state.ex and tokens_gen.ex,
  and triggered on NONE of them - so the
  contrast gate never ran on a PR that
  changed a hue, a background token, or
  the presence palette. D633's law in
  its cheapest form yet: no skip shim,
  no vacuous assertion, just a paths:
  list that omits the subject. Repaired
  in #9379.
[38;5;59m• [0mPDS-D655 - TWO INSTRUMENTS SHIPPED
  CONTRADICTORY PROSE ABOUT THE SAME
  FINDING, IN THE SAME WAVE. pds-door-
  census.sh printed the meter blindness
  as a 236x real/user RATIO; pds-elixir-
  receipt-census.exs refuted exactly
  that on its own runs (113x, then 123x,
  for the SAME fan-out, because real
  counts WAITING). Reconciled in review
  onto the load-independent leaf-metered
  FLOOR. The rule: when a finding ships
  inside more than one instrument, ONE
  owns the sentence and the others cite
  it - the same argument the census's
  own @blind_spot attribute makes, one
  level up.
[38;5;59m• [0mA FLAKE, FILED RATHER THAN SWALLOWED.
  The first full studio-suite run scored
  1 failure in ChatRenderGoldenTest's
  sidebar byte-lock (golden 6554 bytes,
  region 6554 bytes, first diff at 723 -
  a same-length CONTENT SWAP). It passes
  in isolation and passed on re-run of
  the identical tree. Filed as task-
  441f5a5669e0ab6b. A golden that reds
  only sometimes trains reviewers to re-
  run until green, which is this epic's
  own defect class.
[38;5;59m• [0mTHE HOST WAS NOT QUIET, AND THAT
  PROVED D605 BY ACCIDENT. The
  reviewer's census read user cpu 17330
  ms against wall clock 95801 ms on a
  box carrying 24 stray busy-loop
  processes four days old. The wall
  figure is the LOAD; the CPU figure is
  the CODE.

[1;38;5;78mGrade - A-[0m

EARNED, not awarded. What is genuinely
strong: three of four slices closed a
defect that was REAL and proved it by
substitution rather than by reading, and
every one of them refuted part of its
own brief from the artifact - the
palette slice found a THIRD stale
coupling nobody had priced (--fg-muted
was matched for an hsl() both themes
author as var(--muted-text), so that
check had found zero declarations and
could never have run), and the census
slice refused the ratio its dispatch
demanded. The security slice is the
wave's best work: it declined the
obvious fix (widening
Caps.write_capable?/2, which is shared
with the SheetGrid prop and has no
target), declined the obvious predicate
(Access.admits_desk?/3, whose
type/doc_id overwrite IS the bug), and
carries a positive control proving a
legitimate grantee still writes.

What falls short. (1) A whole slice died
between its last stamp and its first
commit and NOTHING in the wave's own
reporting said so - it took a branch-vs-
ledger diff to find a finished
instrument one cycle away from deletion.
That is the sixth consecutive wave to
strand built work, and it is the single
reason this is not an A. (2) Two
instruments shipped contradictory prose
about the same finding in the same wave 
-
a coherence failure the wave had every
opportunity to catch, since one slice
explicitly refuted the other's figure in
its own commit message. (3) The security
slice's shipped non-vacuity test is a
local constant-false STUB, not the
reverted arm; the real revert was run
and recorded in the stamp, but the
artifact that MERGES cannot reproduce it
-
the reviewer had to re-run the mutation
by hand to confirm. (4) Coverage is
honestly partial and says so:
paper_ops/2 and document_op/2 carry the
new arm but are proven by READING, and
the census's ExUnit rider was never run
locally.

No bloat: 489 lines for the security
slice including a 361-line test, 333 for
the census fold, 86 for the palette
repair. Nothing was redesigned; the
reviewer's edits are two latent-hazard
closures and one prose reconciliation.
Tree-tidiness was not pursued and has no
value here.

MERGE ROUND 1 FIRST, IN THIS ORDER:
#9377 (the security fix - it is the only

escalation), then #9379 (it turns the
red Sheet-grid hook unit harness green,
which is currently the only non-advisory

and #9380. Two of the four are owed a

THEN THE DEFERRED ROUND-2 SLICES, EACH

AND ONE NEW STANDING CHORE, from D653:
